Let's design an optimizer `optimize_sequence(p0, time, rng)`:
- Input p0 normalized sum1.
- Precompute `n`, L power of two ≥2n.
- Define `conv_fft(a)`: `fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L); c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:2n-1];` (irfft returns length L; ensure nonnegative small negative set 0). To reduce cost for trials, we can compute c for candidates.
- Initial M, best_p, best_M.
- Define `gradient_smoothed(c, tau)`:
   - sym c: c = 0.5*(c + c[::-1])? c should already symmetric within FFT numerical. We can sym to enforce.
   - M = c.max()

Line search/simple step: Let's define the iteration more concretely.
```
def refine(a0, budget, rng, step=0.35):
    a = a0.copy(); a_prev = a0.copy(); t=1.0; best=a0.copy(); bestM=score(a0); it=0; stuck=0; fails=0
    while time<deadline and it<MAX:
       t_old=t; t=0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4*t*t)); mu=(t_old-1)/t
       y = a + mu*(a-a_prev)
       np.maximum(y,0,out=y); sy=y.sum(); if sy>0 y/=sy
       M_y, c, Pf = conv_score_fft(y,L)
       if M_y < bestM: bestM=M_y; best=y.copy()
       tau = current_tau * max(M_y, tiny); if tau<=0: tau=1e-12
       w = np.exp((c - M_y)/tau)
       wsum = w.sum(); if wsum finite >0: w/=wsum else w = onehot on argsort top
       grad = weighted_gradient(y,Pf,w,K,L)
       grad -= grad.mean()
       # gradient maybe scale? G of smooth c, not score. c scale M. step same. We can use g_norm to adapt step:
       # Lipschitz? Maybe step = step_base / (1 + norm?)
       cand = project_simplex(y - step*grad)
       M_c,_,_ = conv_score_fft(cand,L)
       if M_c < M_y - 1e-15 or M_c < bestM*0.999999:
           # accept
           a_new=cand; # maybe use cand as a for next (not y)
           step = min(step*1.02, 5.0)
           stuck=0; fails=max(0,fails-1)
       else:
           # try half step
           cand2 = project_simplex(y - 0.5*step*grad)
           M_c2,...
           if M_c2 < M_y - ...: accept with step=0.5*step*1.0
           else:
              step = max(0.01, step*0.6)
              # keep best? Use cand2 if M_c2 < M_c? maybe accept best trial to move
              a_new = cand2 if M_c2 < M_y else y? 
              fails+=1
       a_prev = a; a = a_new; t=1 if fails>3 else t updated? 
       it++
       # periodic restart if stuck
```

Frank-Wolfe:
```
lam = np.ones(m)/m
for fw in range(30):
    R = Hc.T @ lam
    # linear minimizer: s = argmin Hc[s] @ R
    vals = Hc @ R
    s = np.argmin(vals)
    gamma = 2.0/(fw+2)
    lam = (1-gamma)*lam; lam[s]+=gamma
return lam
```

Let's implement top-m optimized gradient per iter. This is a key novelty.

Algorithm `refine_topm`:
Inputs a (sum1), duration, initial step, top_m.
At each iteration:
1. Compute c and M via `convolution_and_score(a or y?)`.
   If using FISTA momentum, gradient should be computed at `y` (interpolant), not a. Top peaks from c_y.
2. If M_y < best, update.
3. Select top m indices: use `np.argpartition(c_y, -m)[-m:]`; ensure includes true max? argpartition ok. Sort not needed. Could include duplicates? no. If m>c.size, all.
4. Build gradients H (m,n): for each index r, k=idx[r]; lo... hi... H[r,lo:hi+1]=2*p[k-lo : k-hi-1 : -1] (with p=y). Note p length n.
5. Center each gradient row (optional): `H -= H.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)`. The residual ∑λ H_i centered; the all-ones part irrelevant. Using centered in FW minimizes projected norm.
6. Solve λ via FW:
   - m could be 1: λ=[1].
   - Initialize λ uniform or previous λ? Since peak set changes, set uniform. Or if peak set same, carry? Simpler uniform.
   - For it in range(fw_iters):
       R = Hc.T @ lam
       # Frobenius norm maybe; if norm² < tol break.
       vals = Hc @ R
       s = argmin(vals)
       gamma = 2/(it+2)
       lam = lam*(1-gamma); lam[s]=lam[s]+gamma? More stable: `lam = (1-gamma)*lam; lam[s] += gamma`.
   - Normalize lam sum1.
   G = Hc @ lam  # aggregate centered gradient
   (No need subtract mean after; already centered rows, sum of centered =centered; but due numerical subtract G.mean())
7. Gradient step: `z = y - step*G`. But G is gradient of raw c? Yes ∂c = H rows (with 2). Centered removes constant; step scaled.
8. Project z to simplex: a_new=project_simplex(z).
9. Evaluate M_new (conv). Compare to M_y, best.
10. Step/momentum update and restarts.

Projection simplex implementation efficient:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    n=v.size
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.arange(1,n+1)
    cond = u*rho > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not cond.any(): return np.full(n,1.0/n)
    i = np.where(cond)[0][-1]
    theta = (cssv[i]-1.0)/(i+1.0)
    w = v - theta
    np.maximum(w,0,out=w)
    # normalize for numerical
    sw=w.sum()
    if sw <=0: return np.full(n,1.0/n)
    w /= sw  # Actually projection formula already sums 1; renormalize tiny error
    return w
```
This subtracts theta; if v sum not 1, projection still works? Standard assumes target sum1 but works for any v. It projects onto sum=1. Renormalize may slightly distort but okay. Could avoid renorm if within 1e-12? But renorm fine.

def project_simplex_inplace(v):
    # v is a writable array, will be projected
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    ...

`convolution_fft` function:
```
def convolution_fft(a, nfft):
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, n=nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, n=nfft)
    return c, fa
```
But note: if a sum1 and n large, c may have tiny negative. We clip in score:
```
def max_convolution(a, nfft):
    c, fa = convolution_fft(a,nfft)
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c.max(), c, fa
```
For score only, can avoid returning c if not needed. In refine, need c for weights; `max_convolution` returns c.Pf.
For final candidate fast_score, we can use `max_convolution` then score. Since normalization sum1, score=2nM; for general, divide by sum².

def generate_candidates(n, rng, height_sequence_1):
    cands=[]
    def add(arr): ...
    # given
    try: arr=np.asarray(height_sequence_1,float); arr=np.clip(arr,0,None); if arr.size>=2 and arr.sum()>0: add(arr)
    except: pass
    add(np.ones(n)/n)
    # power left/right alpha
    i=np.arange(n)
    for alpha in (0.45,0.5,0.55):
       p=1.0/(i+1.0)**alpha; add(p)
       p=1.0/(n-1.0-i)**alpha; add(p)
    # binom and transforms
    b=binomial_half(n); add(b)
    add(b[::-1])
    # power of binom
    for q in (0.75,1.25): add(b**q)
    # modulated: b*(0.75+0.25*i/(n-1))
    t=i/(n-1) if n>1 else 0; add(b*(0.75+0.25*t)); add(b*(0.25+0.75*t))
    # random smooth? maybe skip; if time.
    return cands
```

Detailed `refine_smooth` implementation:
```
def refine_smooth(a0, budget, rng, tau_start=0.05, tau_end=0.002, step_init=0.5):
    if budget <= 2: return a0, scoreM(a0)
    n=len(a0); K=2*n-2; K1=2*n-1; L=get_nfft(n)
    a = normalize(a0).copy(); a_prev=a.copy(); t=1.0; step=step_init
    best=a.copy(); bestM=fast_M(a)
    wr=np.zeros(L)
    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget
    it=0; stuck=0; fail_count=0; duration=budget
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       now=time.monotonic()
       t_old=t; t=0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4*t*t)); mu=(t_old-1)/t
       y = a + mu*(a-a_prev)
       np.maximum(y,0,out=y)
       sy=y.sum()
       if sy < 1e-15: y=a.copy()
       else: y /= sy
       c,Pf = convolution_fft(y,L)
       np.maximum(c,0,out=c)
       M = float(c.max())
       if M < bestM:
          bestM=M; best=y.copy() if M from y? Actually y may not be final? feasible. Update best to y copy.
          it_since_best=0
       else: it_since_best += 1
       # tau
       elapsed_frac = (now - start_time)/duration? Need start_time stored.
       tau_rel = tau_start * (tau_end/tau_start)**min(1.0, (now-start_time)/duration)
       tau = max(1e-15, tau_rel * M)
       # weights
       ws = np.exp((c - M)/tau)
       wsum = ws.sum()
       if not finite or wsum<=0: ws.fill(0); ws[int(np.argmax(c))]=1; wsum=1
       ws /= wsum
       wr[:K1] = ws[::-1]; # beyond previously zero
       Rw = np.fft.rfft(wr)
       C = np.fft.irfft(Rw*Pf, n=L)
       G = 2.0 * C[K::-1][:n]
       G -= G.mean()
       # if G too small, maybe use top one peak gradient? If gnorm tiny, break/restart
       gnorm2 = float(np.dot(G,G))
       if gnorm2 < 1e-30:
           # use exact gradient of max peak(s) uniform top few? or break
           # pick top 8 uniform gradient by loop
       # step
       z = y - step*G
       project_simplex_inplace(z)
       Mc = max_convolution_value(z,L)
       if Mc < bestM*0.999999? update best maybe
       if Mc < M + 1e-16 and Mc < M + 1e-15? accepted if not much worse
          if Mc < M: step=min(step*1.05,5); fail=0; t=...; a_prev=a; a=z; it_since_best? 
          else: # stable
             step=min(step*1.001,5); a_prev=a; a=z; fail=max(0,fail-1)
       else:
          step=max(step*0.65,1e-4); fail+=1; a_prev=a; # keep a
          t=1.0
       # if fail>5: maybe accept y? a_prev=a; a=y? To maintain progress? Could set a=a_prev? Hmm.
       # Periodic restart if it_since_best > max(3000, time threshold)
       if it_since_best > max(3000, int(duration)): perturb
       it+=1
```
Need `start_time` for tau; store at function start.

Evaluation of Mc: function `max_conv_value(a,L)` returns c.max after FFT and clip. But we discard c. Use:
```
def max_conv_value(a,L):
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,n=L); c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,n=L); np.maximum(c,0,out=c); return float(c.max())
```
This recomputes FFT. In smooth step, we already did for y; for z one extra.

Projection: `z` is a new array from `y - step*G`; `project_simplex_inplace(z)`. If rejected and we keep a, z discarded.

Momentum update: If accepted, `a_prev = a_old; a = z; t = t_new`. We already computed `t` before. If rejected, set `a_prev = a_old`? Let's manage variables carefully:
At start of loop, old_a = a, old_prev=a_prev. We compute y from old_a and old_prev. If accepted new z:
   a_prev = old_a; a = z; t = t_new (already updated t variable).
If rejected:
   a_prev = old_a.copy()? `a` remains old_a. Set `a_prev = old_a.copy()` to make momentum zero next? Actually y = a + mu*(a-a_prev). If a_prev=a, momentum zero. So set `a_prev = a.copy()` (or old_a). Since a is old_a, set `a_prev[:] = a` maybe. Do `a_prev = a.copy()` to avoid alias? t reset 1.0.
Good.

In accepted, `t` was updated at top; keep it. If multiple accepted, FISTA t grows; can cause instability. Optionally cap t at e.g., 5000. Standard. `if t > 5000: t=1.0`? For nonconvex, large momentum bad. Cap at 1000.

Restart perturbation:
```
def perturb_normalize(a, scale, rng):
    p=a.copy(); p += rng.normal(0,scale,size=n); p=np.maximum(p,0); s=p.sum(); if s>0:p/=s; else:p=a.copy(); return p
```

`refine_topm` similar but uses topm gradient. Let's implement `compute_top_gradient(y, idx, H, m, n)` maybe separate to avoid nested. It fills H and returns G. But H preallocated in refine. It can be a function top-level:
```
def fill_top_gradients(p, idx, H, m, n):
    H[:m] = 0.0
    for r in range(m):
       k = int(idx[r])
       lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
       hi = min(n - 1, k)
       if lo <= hi:
           H[r, lo:hi+1] = 2.0 * p[k - lo : k - hi - 1 : -1]
    # center in place
    row_means = H[:m].mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)
    H[:m] -= row_means
```
Be careful slice `k-hi-1` if hi = k? stop=-1. If lo = k-n+1 and hi=n-1, stop = k-(n-1)-1 = k-n. For k large, e.g., n=5,k=8, lo=4,hi=4, start=4, stop=3? k-hi-1=3; p[4:3:-1]=[p4], correct. If hi = lo? stop=start-1. Good.

Top gradient solve:
```
def solve_top_lambda(H, m, fw_iters, prev_lam, prev_idx_sorted, idx_sorted):
    if m==1: lam=np.ones(1)
    else:
       if prev_lam is not None and np.array_equal(prev_idx_sorted, idx_sorted):
           lam = prev_lam.astype(float).copy()
       else:
           lam = np.full(m, 1.0/m)
       # options: ensure lam sum1
       for it in range(fw_iters):
          R = H.T @ lam
          rnorm2 = float(R @ R)
          if rnorm2 < 1e-26: break
          vals = H @ R
          s = int(np.argmin(vals))
          gamma = 2.0 / (it + 3.0)
          lam = (1.0 - gamma) * lam
          lam[s] += gamma
       lam /= lam.sum()
    return lam
```
Use H C-contiguous. `H.T @ lam` with m small. Good.

idx_sorted=np.sort(idx)

Thus final code phases:
1. Candidate eval.
2. `refine_smooth` for ~50% time (tau annealed). This uses smooth aggregate gradient via FFT (cost transforms, no H). It should make initial improvement.
3. `refine_topm` for ~40% time. It uses top-m λ gradient and smooth fallback. This should polish.
4. Remaining: optional smooth or topm from perturbed best if time.

Let's refine `refine_smooth` code in detail with variables.
```
def refine_smooth(a0, budget, rng, tau_start=0.03, tau_end=0.0015, step_init=0.6):
    if budget <= 1: return a0, fast_M(a0)
    n = len(a0); L=get_nfft(n); K=2*n-2; K1=2*n-1
    a = normalize_copy(a0)
    if not finite: a=np.ones(n)/n
    a_prev = a.copy(); t=1.0; step=step_init
    best = a.copy(); bestM = fast_M(a,L)
    start = time.monotonic(); deadline = start + float(budget)
    wr = np.zeros(L)
    it=0; stuck=0; fail=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       t_old = t
       t_new = 0.5*(1.0+math.sqrt(1.0+4.0*t_old*t_old))
       if t_new > 100.0: t_new = 100.0
       mu = (t_old - 1.0)/t_new
       y = a + mu*(a - a_prev)
       np.maximum(y, 0.0, out=y)
       sy = y.sum()
       if sy < 1e-15:
           y = a.copy()
       else:
           y /= sy
       c, Pf = convolution_fft(y, L)
       np.maximum(c,0.0,out=c)
       M = float(c.max())
       if M < bestM:
           bestM = M; best = y.copy(); stuck=0
       else:
           stuck += 1
       elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
       frac = elapsed / max(1e-12, float(budget))
       if frac > 1: frac=1
       tau_rel = tau_start * (tau_end/tau_start) ** frac
       tau = max(1e-18, tau_rel * M)
       # weights
       np.exp((c - M) / tau, out=c_weights?)
```
We need a buffer for weights to avoid allocating each iter. Use `wbuf = np.empty(K1)` in refine. But `np.exp` can write to `wbuf` if we first copy c? c length K1. Do:
```
       wbuf = ...
       denom = tau
       np.subtract(c, M, out=wbuf)
       np.divide(wbuf, denom, out=wbuf)
       np.exp(wbuf, out=wbuf)
       wsum = float(wbuf.sum())
       if not finite or wsum<=0: wbuf.fill(0); wbuf[int(np.argmax(c))]=1; wsum=1
       wbuf /= wsum
```
Allocating `np.exp` out avoids new. Good.
But after `wbuf` normalized, we need reversed into wr: `wr[:K1] = wbuf[::-1]`. `wbuf[::-1]` view; assignment copies. Since L > K1, beyond zero. Good.

```

Now, raw top uniform gradient helper for fallback:
```
def raw_top_gradient(c, p, m=8, n):
   idx=np.argpartition(c,-m)[-m:]
   G=np.zeros(n)
   for k in idx:
      lo... hi... G[lo:hi+1] += 2.0 * p[k-lo: k-hi-1: -1]
   G -= G.mean(); return G
```
This loop small. Use when G norm tiny.

Define `get_nfft(n)`:
```
def get_nfft(n):
    need = max(16, 3*n - 1)
    size = 1
    while size < need: size <<=1
    return size
```
For n=4806, 3*n-1=14417 ->16384.

`normalize_copy(a)`:
```
def normalize_copy(a):
    a=np.array(a,dtype=np.float64, copy=True).ravel()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    np.maximum(a,0.0,out=a)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
       return np.full(a.size, 1.0/a.size)
    a /= s
    return a
```

`project_simplex_inplace(v)`:
```
def project_simplex_inplace(v):
    n=v.size
    if n==0: return v
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    idx=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u*idx > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not cond.any():
        v[:] = 1.0/n
        return v
    i = int(np.where(cond)[0][-1])
    theta = (cssv[i] - 1.0)/(i+1.0)
    v -= theta
    np.maximum(v,0.0,out=v)
    s=v.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
       v[:] = 1.0/n
    else:
       v *= 1.0/s
    return v
```
Potential issue: If original v sum not 1, this projects to sum 1, okay. But if v has large positive/negative, sort. Good.

`convolution_fft(a, L)` returns c,Pf:
```
def convolution_fft(a, L):
    Pf = np.fft.rfft(a, n=L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(Pf * Pf, n=L)
    return c, Pf
```

`max_conv_value(a,L)`:
```
def max_conv_value(a,L):
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,n=L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,n=L)
    np.maximum(c,0.0,out=c)
    return float(c.max())
```
Score from M: `2*n*M` if sum1.

`raw_top_gradient(c,p,m=8)`.

`fill_top_gradients(p, idx, H, m, n)` as above. Note p may be `y`; idx length m. H rows centered. Use `H[:m] = 0.0`; loop.

`solve_top_lambda(H, m, prev_lam, fw_iters)`:
```
def solve_top_lambda(H, m, prev_lam, fw_iters):
    if m == 1:
       return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if prev_lam is not None and prev_lam.size == m:
       lam = prev_lam.astype(np.float64, copy=True)
       lam /= lam.sum()
    else:
       lam = np.full(m, 1.0/m, dtype=np.float64)
    for it in range(fw_iters):
       R = H.T @ lam
       rnorm2 = float(R @ R)
       if rnorm2 < 1e-28:
           break
       vals = H @ R
       s = int(np.argmin(vals))
       gamma = 2.0 / (it + 3.0)
       # avoid gamma >1
       lam = (1.0 - gamma) * lam
       lam[s] += gamma
    lam /= lam.sum()
    return lam
```

`smooth_gradient(c, M, Pf, L, K, n, tau, wr, wbuf)`:
Need wbuf length K1; we can allocate in function or pass. Since topm has wbuf. It returns G.
```
def smooth_gradient(c, M, Pf, L, K, n, tau, wr, wbuf):
    np.subtract(c, M, out=wbuf)
    np.divide(wbuf, tau, out=wbuf)
    np.exp(wbuf, out=wbuf)
    s=float(wbuf.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       wbuf.fill(0.0); wbuf[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0; s=1.0
    wbuf *= 1.0/s
    wr[:K+1] = wbuf[::-1]
    Rw = np.fft.rfft(wr)
    C = np.fft.irfft(Rw * Pf, n=L)
    G = 2.0 * C[K::-1][:n]
    G -= G.mean()
    return G
```
Need ensure wr beyond K+1 zero. It is zero initially; assignment only up to K+1. But if we use smooth fallback multiple times, wr[:K+1] overwritten; beyond remain zero. Good. If K+1 not full L, zeros remain if never set. Good.

`refine_smooth` uses its own `wbuf`, `wr`. It could call `smooth_gradient` but that expects wbuf length K1. We'll implement inline to reuse.

`refine_smooth` more code. Need local `start = time.monotonic()`. Use `budget = min(budget, (total_deadline?))`? It gets budget seconds. Ensure not exceed. In main, pass limited duration.

def list_score_if_possible(lst):

       v = float(evaluate_sequence(lst))

       pass
    return np.inf

    except Exception:
       arr=None

    print final
    return best_final

Pseudo code for `refine_smooth`:
```
def refine_smooth(a0, budget, rng, tau_start=0.04, tau_end=0.002, step_init=0.45):
    a0 = normalize_copy(a0)
    n = len(a0)
    if n < 2: return a0, 0.0
    L = get_nfft(n)
    K = 2*n - 2
    K1 = 2*n - 1
    a = ...
    a_prev = a.copy()
    t = 1.0; step=step_init
    best=a.copy(); bestM = max_conv_value(a,L)
    wr=np.zeros(L)
    wbuf=np.empty(K1)
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+float(budget)
    it=0; stuck=0; fail=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       t_old=t
       t_new=0.5*(1+math.sqrt(1+4*t_old*t_old))
       if t_new > 100: t_new=100
       mu=(t_old-1)/t_new
       y = a + mu*(a-a_prev)
       np.maximum(y,0,out=y)
       sy = y.sum()
       if sy < 1e-15: y = a.copy()
       else: y /= sy
       c, Pf = convolution_fft(y,L)
       c_seg = c[:K1]
       np.maximum(c_seg,0,out=c_seg)
       M = float(c_seg.max())
       if M < bestM:
          bestM=M; best=y.copy(); stuck=0
       else: stuck += 1
       elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
       frac = elapsed / max(1e-12, float(budget))
       if frac > 1: frac = 1
       tau_rel = tau_start * (tau_end / tau_start) ** frac
       tau = max(1e-18, tau_rel * M)
       np.subtract(c_seg, M, out=wbuf)
       np.divide(wbuf, tau, out=wbuf)
       np.exp(wbuf, out=wbuf)
       wsum = float(wbuf.sum())
       if not math.isfinite(wsum) or wsum <= 0:
          wbuf.fill(0)
          wbuf[int(np.argmax(c_seg))] = 1
          wsum = 1
       wbuf *= 1/wsum
       wr[:K1] = wbuf[::-1]
       Rw = np.fft.rfft(wr)
       C = np.fft.irfft(Rw * Pf, n=L)
       G = 2.0 * C[K::-1][:n]
       G -= G.mean()
       gnorm2 = float(np.dot(G,G))
       if gnorm2 < 1e-30:
          G = raw_top_gradient(c_seg, y, 8, n)
       z, Mc = projected_step(y, G, step, L)
       if Mc < bestM:
          bestM=Mc; best=z.copy(); stuck=0
       if Mc < M + 1e-14 or Mc < bestM*0.999999? (bestM already updated)
          accept...
       else:
          z2, Mc2 = projected_step(y, G, 0.5*step, L)
          if Mc2 < bestM: update
          if Mc2 < M + 1e-14:
             accept half reset t=1
          else:
             step=max(step*0.6,1e-4); fail+=1; a_prev=a.copy(); t=1.0
       it += 1
       # restart
       if stuck > max(2500, int(float(budget)/2)) and time.monotonic() < deadline - 30:
           scale = 0.025 / math.sqrt(n)
           p = best.copy()
           p += rng.normal(0.0, scale, size=n)
           np.maximum(p,0,out=p)
           ps=p.sum()
           if ps > 1e-300: p /= ps
           else: p = best.copy()
           a=p.copy(); a_prev=p.copy(); t=1.0; step=step_init; fail=0; stuck=0
    # final: evaluate a maybe
    Ma = max_conv_value(a,L)
    if Ma < bestM: bestM=Ma; best=a.copy()
    return best,bestM
```

`raw_top_gradient`:
```
def raw_top_gradient(c, p, m, n):
    kl = len(c)
    if m >= kl: idx=np.arange(kl)
    else: idx=np.argpartition(c,-m)[-m:]
    G=np.zeros(n)
    for k in idx:
       k=int(k)
       lo=...; hi=...
       G[lo:hi+1] += 2.0*p[k-lo:k-hi-1:-1]
    G -= G.mean()
    return G
```
If m large and idx unsorted, okay.

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative step-function sequence minimizing

    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a))^2

using a two-stage accelerated projected gradient method.

Key ideas:
1. Work on the probability simplex (normalize sum = 1), so the objective is
   2*n*max(conv(a,a)).
2. Use FFT to evaluate full autoconvolution quickly.
3. Primary phase: smooth the max with a softmax weight over convolution
   coefficients and compute the full weighted subgradient by an FFT-based
   correlation (no per-peak Python loop).
4. Polishing phase: take the top-m largest convolution peaks, compute their
   gradient rows, and find a better subgradient convex combination by
   Frank-Wolfe (closest point to zero in the convex hull of centered
   gradients). This avoids the bias of a uniform top-k average.
5. Multiple warm starts / restores and adaptive step/momentum restarts keep
   the search robust inside the 2400 s budget.
"""

import math
import time

import numpy as np


def get_nfft(n):
    """FFT length large enough for p*p and for weighted-gradient correlations."""
    need = max(16, 3 * int(n) - 1)
    size = 1
    while size < need:
        size <<= 1
    return size


def normalize_copy(a):
    """Return a non-negative normalized copy of a."""
    a = np.array(a, dtype=np.float64, copy=True).ravel()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    np.maximum(a, 0.0, out=a)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / max(1, a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    a *= 1.0 / s
    return a


def project_simplex_inplace(v):
    """Project v in-place onto {x >= 0, sum(x) = 1}."""
    n = int(v.size)
    if n == 0:
        return v

    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    rho = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u * rho > (cssv - 1.0)

    if not cond.any():
        v[:] = 1.0 / n
        return v

    i = int(np.where(cond)[0][-1])
    theta = (cssv[i] - 1.0) / float(i + 1)
    v -= theta
    np.maximum(v, 0.0, out=v)

    s = float(v.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        v[:] = 1.0 / n
    else:
        v *= 1.0 / s
    return v


def convolution_fft(a, nfft):
    """Full autoconvolution by FFT. Returns (conv_full_length, fft_a)."""
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, n=nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, n=nfft)
    return c, fa


def max_conv_value(a, nfft):
    """max of full autoconvolution of a (assumes a is non-negative, any scale)."""
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, n=nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, n=nfft)
    k1 = 2 * int(a.size) - 1
    seg = c[:k1]
    np.maximum(seg, 0.0, out=seg)
    return float(seg.max())


def raw_top_gradient(c, p, m, n):
    """Uniform convex combination of the top-m raw gradient rows."""
    k1 = int(c.size)
    if m >= k1:
        idx = np.arange(k1, dtype=np.int64)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -m)[-m:]

    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for kk in idx:
        k = int(kk)
        lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if lo <= hi:
            g[lo:hi + 1] += 2.0 * p[k - lo:k - hi - 1:-1]
    g -= g.mean()
    return g


def projected_step(y, g, step, nfft):
    """One projected gradient step; returns (new_point, new_max_conv)."""
    z = y - step * g
    project_simplex_inplace(z)
    mz = max_conv_value(z, nfft)
    return z, mz


def fill_top_gradients(p, idx, H, m, n):
    """
    Fill H[:m] with centered gradient rows of top peaks idx.

    derivative of c_k wrt p_j is 2*p_{k-j} when 0 <= k-j < n.
    """
    H[:m] = 0.0
    for r in range(m):
        k = int(idx[r])
        lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if lo <= hi:
            H[r, lo:hi + 1] = 2.0 * p[k - lo:k - hi - 1:-1]

    H[:m] -= H[:m].mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)
    return H[:m]


def solve_top_lambda(H, m, prev_lam, fw_iters):
    """
    Frank-Wolfe for
        min_lambda || H @ lambda ||^2  s.t. lambda >= 0, sum(lambda)=1.
    H rows are already centered.
    """
    if m == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    if prev_lam is not None and prev_lam.size == m:
        lam = prev_lam.astype(np.float64, copy=True)
        s = float(lam.sum())
        if s <= 1e-300 or not math.isfinite(s):
            lam = np.full(m, 1.0 / m, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            lam *= 1.0 / s
    else:
        lam = np.full(m, 1.0 / m, dtype=np.float64)

    for it in range(fw_iters):
        R = H.T @ lam
        rnorm2 = float(R @ R)
        if rnorm2 < 1e-28:
            break

        vals = H @ R
        s = int(np.argmin(vals))
        gamma = 2.0 / (it + 3.0)
        lam = (1.0 - gamma) * lam
        lam[s] += gamma

    s = float(lam.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        lam = np.full(m, 1.0 / m, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        lam *= 1.0 / s
    return lam


def smooth_gradient(c, mval, pf, nfft, K, n, tau, wr, wbuf):
    """
    Smoothed exact-gradient average:
        G = sum_k w_k * grad(c_k),
    where w_k = softmax((c_k - max(c)) / tau).
    Computed by one FFT correlation.
    """
    np.subtract(c, mval, out=wbuf)
    np.divide(wbuf, tau, out=wbuf)
    np.exp(wbuf, out=wbuf)

    s = float(wbuf.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        wbuf.fill(0.0)
        wbuf[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        s = 1.0
    wbuf *= 1.0 / s

    wr[:K + 1] = wbuf[::-1]
    wrf = np.fft.rfft(wr)
    corr = np.fft.irfft(wrf * pf, n=nfft)

    g = 2.0 * corr[K::-1][:n]
    g -= g.mean()
    return g


def binomial_half(n):
    """Normalized coefficients of (1 - z)^(-1/2), truncated to length n."""
    b = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        b[i] = b[i - 1] * (2.0 * i - 1.0) / (2.0 * i)
    s = float(b.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    b *= 1.0 / s
    return b


def append_candidate(cands, arr):
    """Append a normalized finite non-negative candidate if valid."""
    try:
        a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return

    if a.size == 0:
        return

    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    np.maximum(a, 0.0, out=a)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return

    a *= 1.0 / s
    cands.append(a)


def generate_candidates(n, given_arr, rng):
    """Create a diversified set of normalized starting points."""
    cands = []

    # Given previous construction, if available and finite.
    append_candidate(cands, given_arr)

    # Uniform.
    append_candidate(cands, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)

    # One-sided powers.
    for alpha in (0.45, 0.50, 0.55):
        append_candidate(cands, 1.0 / (i + 1.0) ** alpha)
        append_candidate(cands, 1.0 / (n - i) ** alpha)

    # Critical square-root Taylor shape and simple transforms.
    b = binomial_half(n)
    append_candidate(cands, b)
    append_candidate(cands, b[::-1])
    append_candidate(cands, b ** 0.75)
    append_candidate(cands, b ** 1.25)

    if n > 1:
        t = i / float(n - 1)
        append_candidate(cands, b * (0.75 + 0.25 * t))
        append_candidate(cands, b * (0.25 + 0.75 * t))
        append_candidate(cands, b * (0.75 + 0.25 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * i / float(n))))

    # A few random smooth starts for extra diversity.
    for _ in range(3):
        x = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
        # Light exponential smoothing.
        kernel = np.exp(-np.arange(0, 21, dtype=np.float64) / 8.0)
        x = np.convolve(x, kernel / kernel.sum(), mode="same")
        append_candidate(cands, x)

    # If something went wrong, ensure at least one candidate exists.
    if not cands:
        cands.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    return cands


def refine_smooth(a0, budget, rng, tau_start=0.04, tau_end=0.0018, step_init=0.45):
    """
    Fast robust FISTA-like phase using all close-to-max coefficients through
    an FFT-computed softmax-weighted gradient.
    """
    a0 = normalize_copy(a0)
    n = int(a0.size)
    if n < 2:
        return a0.copy(), 0.0

    budget = float(max(1.0, budget))
    nfft = get_nfft(n)
    K = 2 * n - 2
    K1 = 2 * n - 1

    a = a0.copy()
    a_prev = a0.copy()
    t = 1.0
    step = float(step_init)

    best = a.copy()
    bestM = max_conv_value(a, nfft)

    wr = np.zeros(nfft, dtype=np.float64)
    wbuf = np.empty(K1, dtype=np.float64)

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + budget

    it = 0
    stuck = 0
    fail = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        old_a = a
        old_prev = a_prev

        t_old = t
        t_new = 0.5 * (1.0 + math.sqrt(1.0 + 4.0 * t_old * t_old))
        if t_new > 100.0:
            t_new = 100.0
        mu = (t_old - 1.0) / t_new

        y = old_a + mu * (old_a - old_prev)
        np.maximum(y, 0.0, out=y)
        syy = float(y.sum())
        if syy < 1e-15:
            y = old_a.copy()
        else:
            y *= 1.0 / syy

        c, pf = convolution_fft(y, nfft)
        cseg = c[:K1]
        np.maximum(cseg, 0.0, out=cseg)
        M = float(cseg.max())

        if M < bestM:
            bestM = M
            best = y.copy()
            stuck = 0
        else:
            stuck += 1

        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        frac = elapsed / budget
        if frac > 1.0:
            frac = 1.0
        tau_rel = tau_start * (tau_end / tau_start) ** frac
        tau = max(1e-18, tau_rel * M)

        g = smooth_gradient(cseg, M, pf, nfft, K, n, tau, wr, wbuf)

        if float(np.dot(g, g)) < 1e-30:
            g = raw_top_gradient(cseg, y, 8, n)

        z, mz = projected_step(y, g, step, nfft)
        if mz < bestM:
            bestM = mz
            best = z.copy()
            stuck = 0

        accepted = False
        if mz < bestM or mz <= M + 1e-14:
            if mz < M:
                step = min(step * 1.04, 4.0)
            else:
                step = min(step * 1.005, 4.0)
            a_prev = old_a
            a = z
            t = t_new
            fail = max(0, fail - 1)
            accepted = True
        else:
            z2, mz2 = projected_step(y, g, 0.5 * step, nfft)
            if mz2 < bestM:
                bestM = mz2
                best = z2.copy()
                stuck = 0
            if mz2 < bestM or mz2 <= M + 1e-14:
                a_prev = old_a
                a = z2
                t = 1.0
                step = max(step * 0.75, 0.05)
                fail += 1
                accepted = True
            else:
                step = max(step * 0.6, 1e-4)
                fail += 1
                a_prev = old_a.copy()
                t = 1.0

        it += 1

        if fail > 8:
            a = best.copy()
            a_prev = a.copy()
            t = 1.0
            step = max(0.1, step_init * 0.5)
            fail = 0
            stuck = max(stuck, 500)

        if stuck > max(2500, int(budget / 2.0)) and time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
            scale = 0.025 / math.sqrt(float(n))
            p = best.copy()
            p += rng.normal(0.0, scale, size=n)
            np.maximum(p, 0.0, out=p)
            ps = float(p.sum())
            if ps > 1e-300:
                p *= 1.0 / ps
            else:
                p = best.copy()
            a = p.copy()
            a_prev = p.copy()
            t = 1.0
            step = step_init
            fail = 0
            stuck = 0

    ma = max_conv_value(a, nfft)
    if ma < bestM:
        bestM = ma
        best = a.copy()

    return best, bestM


def refine_topm(a0, budget, rng, top_init=24, top_max=96):
    """
    Polishing phase using an optimized subgradient convex combination over
    top-m active convolution peaks.
    """
    a0 = normalize_copy(a0)
    n = int(a0.size)
    if n < 2:
        return a0.copy(), 0.0

    budget = float(max(1.0, budget))
    nfft = get_nfft(n)
    K = 2 * n - 2
    K1 = 2 * n - 1

    max_peaks = 2 * n - 1
    top_init = max(1, min(int(top_init), max_peaks))
    top_max = max(top_init, min(int(top_max), max_peaks))

    a = a0.copy()
    a_prev = a0.copy()
    t = 1.0
    step = 0.55

    best = a.copy()
    bestM = max_conv_value(a, nfft)

    H = np.zeros((top_max, n), dtype=np.float64)
    wr = np.zeros(nfft, dtype=np.float64)
    wbuf = np.empty(K1, dtype=np.float64)

    prev_idx = None
    prev_lam = None
    prev_m = -1
    lam_counter = 10**9

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + budget

    it = 0
    stuck = 0
    fail = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        old_a = a
        old_prev = a_prev

        t_old = t
        t_new = 0.5 * (1.0 + math.sqrt(1.0 + 4.0 * t_old * t_old))
        if t_new > 100.0:
            t_new = 100.0
        mu = (t_old - 1.0) / t_new

        y = old_a + mu * (old_a - old_prev)
        np.maximum(y, 0.0, out=y)
        syy = float(y.sum())
        if syy < 1e-15:
            y = old_a.copy()
        else:
            y *= 1.0 / syy

        c, pf = convolution_fft(y, nfft)
        cseg = c[:K1]
        np.maximum(cseg, 0.0, out=cseg)
        M = float(cseg.max())

        if M < bestM:
            bestM = M
            best = y.copy()
            stuck = 0
        else:
            stuck += 1

        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        frac = elapsed / budget
        if frac < 0.0:
            frac = 0.0
        if frac > 1.0:
            frac = 1.0

        top_m = int(round(top_init + (top_max - top_init) * frac))
        top_m = max(1, min(top_m, max_peaks))

        if top_m >= K1:
            idx_sorted = np.arange(K1, dtype=np.int64)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(cseg, -top_m)[-top_m:]
            idx_sorted = np.sort(idx)

        Hm = fill_top_gradients(y, idx_sorted, H, top_m, n)

        same_shape = (prev_m == top_m)
        same_idx = same_shape and (prev_idx is not None and np.array_equal(prev_idx, idx_sorted))

        if same_idx:
            lam_counter += 1
            if lam_counter > 25:
                lam = solve_top_lambda(Hm, top_m, prev_lam, 10)
                prev_lam = lam.copy()
                lam_counter = 0
            else:
                lam = prev_lam
        else:
            warm = prev_lam if (prev_m == top_m and same_shape) else None
            lam = solve_top_lambda(Hm, top_m, warm, 22 if warm is None else 12)
            prev_lam = lam.copy()
            prev_idx = idx_sorted.copy()
            prev_m = top_m
            lam_counter = 0

        g = Hm @ lam
        g -= g.mean()
        gnorm2 = float(np.dot(g, g))

        if gnorm2 < 1e-30 or fail > 5 or (stuck > 2000 and (it % 10 == 0)):
            tau = max(1e-18, 0.02 * M)
            gs = smooth_gradient(cseg, M, pf, nfft, K, n, tau, wr, wbuf)
            gsnorm2 = float(np.dot(gs, gs))
            if gsnorm2 > max(1e-30, 0.5 * max(gnorm2, 1e-30)):
                g = gs

        if float(np.dot(g, g)) < 1e-30:
            g = raw_top_gradient(cseg, y, min(16, K1), n)

        z, mz = projected_step(y, g, step, nfft)
        if mz < bestM:
            bestM = mz
            best = z.copy()
            stuck = 0

        accepted = False
        if mz < bestM or mz <= M + 1e-14:
            if mz < M:
                step = min(step * 1.05, 4.0)
            else:
                step = min(step * 1.005, 4.0)
            a_prev = old_a
            a = z
            t = t_new
            fail = max(0, fail - 1)
            lam_counter = min(lam_counter, 10)
            accepted = True
        else:
            z2, mz2 = projected_step(y, g, 0.5 * step, nfft)
            if mz2 < bestM:
                bestM = mz2
                best = z2.copy()
                stuck = 0
            if mz2 < bestM or mz2 <= M + 1e-14:
                a_prev = old_a
                a = z2
                t = 1.0
                step = max(step * 0.7, 0.05)
                fail += 1
                accepted = True
            else:
                step = max(step * 0.6, 1e-4)
                fail += 1
                a_prev = old_a.copy()
                t = 1.0

        it += 1

        if fail > 8:
            a = best.copy()
            a_prev = a.copy()
            t = 1.0
            step = 0.35
            fail = 0
            stuck = max(stuck, 500)

        if stuck > max(2500, int(budget / 2.0)) and time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
            scale = 0.02 / math.sqrt(float(n))
            p = best.copy()
            p += rng.normal(0.0, scale, size=n)
            np.maximum(p, 0.0, out=p)
            ps = float(p.sum())
            if ps > 1e-300:
                p *= 1.0 / ps
            else:
                p = best.copy()
            a = p.copy()
            a_prev = p.copy()
            t = 1.0
            step = 0.45
            fail = 0
            stuck = 0
            prev_idx = None
            prev_lam = None

    ma = max_conv_value(a, nfft)
    if ma < bestM:
        bestM = ma
        best = a.copy()

    return best, bestM


def list_score_if_possible(seq):
    """Call the provided evaluator safely; return inf on failure."""
    try:
        v = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except NameError:
        return np.inf
    except Exception:
        return np.inf

    if math.isfinite(v):
        return v
    return np.inf


def propose_candidate(budget=2400.0, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search function. Returns the best non-negative list found.

    It starts from the provided height_sequence_1 when available, tries a few
    analytic/parametric instinctions, refines with a smooth FFT-subgradient
    method, then polishes with a top-m optimized subgradient method.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(60.0, float(budget) - 30.0)
    deadline = start + total_budget
    rng = np.random.default_rng(20250528)

    # Recover/decide on length. The known construction length is 4806.
    given = None
    try:
        h = height_sequence_1
        arr = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size >= 2:
            arr[~np.isfinite(arr)] = 0.0
            np.maximum(arr, 0.0, out=arr)
            if float(arr.sum()) > 1e-300:
                # If absurdly large, fall back to the documented length to stay within time.
                if arr.size <= 15000:
                    given = arr
    except Exception:
        given = None

    if given is None:
        n = 4806
        given = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        n = int(given.size)

    nfft = get_nfft(n)

    cands = generate_candidates(n, given, rng)

    scored = []
    for idx, p in enumerate(cands):
        mval = max_conv_value(p, nfft)
        scored.append((mval, idx))
    scored.sort()

    sorted_cands = [cands[i] for _, i in scored]

    best_a = normalize_copy(sorted_cands[0])
    best_m = max_conv_value(best_a, nfft)

    print(f"length={n}, best initial score~{2.0*n*best_m:.8f}", flush=True)

    # Phase 1: smooth robust refinement.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 90.0:
        dur1 = min(rem - 60.0, 0.48 * total_budget)
        if dur1 > 30.0:
            print(f"phase smooth: budget={dur1:.1f}s", flush=True)
            best_a, best_m = refine_smooth(
                best_a,
                dur1,
                rng,
                tau_start=0.04,
                tau_end=0.0018,
                step_init=0.45,
            )
            print(f"after smooth: score~{2.0*n*best_m:.8f}", flush=True)

    # Phase 2: if a second candidate is very close, give it some time.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 120.0 and len(scored) >= 2:
        second_m = float(scored[1][0])
        if second_m <= best_m * 1.02 + 1e-12:
            dur2 = min(rem - 90.0, 0.12 * total_budget)
            if dur2 > 30.0:
                second_a = normalize_copy(sorted_cands[1])
                second_m0 = max_conv_value(second_a, nfft)
                print(f"phase smooth-alt: budget={dur2:.1f}s start~{2.0*n*second_m0:.8f}", flush=True)
                second_a, second_m = refine_smooth(
                    second_a,
                    dur2,
                    rng,
                    tau_start=0.04,
                    tau_end=0.0018,
                    step_init=0.45,
                )
                if second_m < best_m:
                    best_a, best_m = second_a, second_m
                    print(f"alt improved: score~{2.0*n*best_m:.8f}", flush=True)

    # Phase 3: top-m polishing from the current global best.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 90.0:
        dur3 = min(rem - 45.0, 0.38 * total_budget)
        if dur3 > 30.0:
            print(f"phase topm: budget={dur3:.1f}s", flush=True)
            best_a, best_m = refine_topm(
                best_a,
                dur3,
                rng,
                top_init=24,
                top_max=96,
            )
            print(f"after topm: score~{2.0*n*best_m:.8f}", flush=True)

    # Phase 4: if time remains, one more polishing wave with a slightly larger top-m.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 90.0:
        dur4 = rem - 30.0
        if dur4 > 30.0:
            print(f"phase topm2: budget={dur4:.1f}s", flush=True)
            best_a, best_m = refine_topm(
                best_a,
                dur4,
                rng,
                top_init=48,
                top_max=128,
            )
            print(f"after topm2: score~{2.0*n*best_m:.8f}", flush=True)

    # Final safety: convert and choose the best by the real evaluator among a few candidates.
    final_seq = [float(x) for x in best_a]
    final_score = list_score_if_possible(final_seq)

    fallbacks = []
    fallbacks.append(("best", normalize_copy(best_a)))
    fallbacks.append(("uniform", np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)))
    if len(sorted_cands) >= 1:
        fallbacks.append(("initial", normalize_copy(sorted_cands[0])))
    try:
        fb_given = normalize_copy(given)
        fallbacks.append(("given", fb_given))
    except Exception:
        pass

    seen = set()
    for name, fb in fallbacks:
        # Avoid duplicate direct evaluations for identical arrays at coarse level.
        key = (tuple(rb[0]) if rb else None)
        # Use a cheap fingerprint instead of tuple of all floats for speed.
        try:
            fp = (
                float(fb.sum()),
                float(fb.max()),
                float(fb.min() if fb.size else 1.0),
                float(np.dot(fb, np.arange(fb.size, dtype=np.float64))) if fb.size < 20000 else 0.0,
            )
        except Exception:
            fp = None
        if fp is not None and fp in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(fp)

        fseq = [float(x) for x in fb]
        fs = list_score_if_possible(fseq)
        if math.isfinite(fs) and (not math.isfinite(final_score) or fs < final_score):
            final_seq = fseq
            final_score = fs

    if not math.isfinite(final_score):
        # Absolute safe fallback.
        final_seq = [1.0] * n
        final_score = list_score_if_possible(final_seq)

    print(f"=== FINAL: internal={2.0*n*max_conv_value(np.asarray(final_seq), nfft):.8f} evaluator={final_score:.8f} n={n} ===", flush=True)
    return final_seq
```
